<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis for every Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention: its weak globular infinity-groupoids recover the homotopy theory of spaces. We also resolve Henry's pushout conjecture, showing that elementary expansions preserve components and all homotopy groups of cellular infinity-groupoids.

- **claim-elementary-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix any Grothendieck coherator $\mathcal C$. Let $X$ be a cellular $\mathcal C$-infinity-groupoid, let $n\ge0$, and let $a:D_n\to X$ be any cell. In the pushout square $$\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center), node distance=1.25cm and 2.2cm,>=Stealth] \node (a) {\(D_n\)}; \node (b) [right=of a] {\(D_{n+1}\)}; \node (c) [below=of a] {\(X\)}; \node (d) at (b \|- c) {\(X^+\)}; \draw[->] (a) -- node[above] {\(s_n\)} (b); \draw[->] (a) -- node[left] {\(a\)} (c); \draw[->] (b) -- (d); \draw[->] (c) -- node[below] {\(i\)} (d); \end{tikzpicture}$$ where $s_n$ is the source inclusion, the map $i:X\to X^+$ is a weak equivalence.
  - Explanation: For any Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention and any cellular model, attaching a higher disk along its source face to a chosen cell of the model is a weak equivalence. This resolves Henry's pushout conjecture.
- **claim-division-bijection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prelim-division)): Fix an $r$-cell $\gamma$ and $i<r$. Left multiplication $\alpha\mapsto\gamma*_i\alpha$ induces a bijection from the homotopy set at any prescribed input boundary composable with $\gamma$ to the homotopy set at its induced output boundary. The same holds for right multiplication. Empty homotopy sets are allowed.
  - Explanation: Composing with a fixed cell at a level below the top is a bijection between homotopy sets with prescribed boundaries, even when those sets are empty.
- **claim-boundary-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prelim-boundary-change)): The endomorphism homotopy set at any $(r-1)$-cell $u$ is naturally in bijection with the underlying set of $\pi_r(X,x)$, where $x$ is either its source or its target vertex. An arrow $x\to y$ gives bijections between the underlying sets of $\pi_r(X,x)$ and $\pi_r(X,y)$, natural in model maps. If $\pi_r(X;u,v)$ is nonempty, a chosen cell $u\to v$ gives a bijection from the endomorphism set at $u$ to this homotopy set.
  - Explanation: Homotopy sets at an arbitrary boundary are identified naturally with the based homotopy groups, and a change of vertex gives bijections of the underlying sets.
- **claim-weak-equivalence-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-weak-test)): For a model map $f:X\to Y$, the following are equivalent.* 1. *$f\in\mathcal W$.* 2. *It induces a bijection on $\pi_0$ and a bijection $$\pi_r(X;u,v)\longrightarrow\pi_r(Y;fu,fv)$$ for every $r\ge1$ and every parallel pair $u,v$ in $X$.* 3. *For every $r\ge0$, every specified boundary $b:\partial D_r\to X$, and every $r$-cell $y$ of $Y$ with boundary $fb$, there are an $r$-cell $x$ with boundary *exactly* $b$ and an $(r+1)$-cell $fx\to y$ in $Y$.* *In particular, a weak equivalence reflects homotopy between parallel cells, including equality of components for vertices.
  - Explanation: Being a weak equivalence is equivalent to bijectivity on homotopy sets between all parallel pairs and to an exact-boundary lifting property up to a higher cell.
- **claim-weak-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-closure)): The class $\mathcal W$ has two-out-of-three, is closed under retracts, and is closed under filtered colimits of arrows. Consequently a transfinite composite of weak equivalences is a weak equivalence.
  - Explanation: Weak equivalences satisfy two-out-of-three and are stable under retracts, filtered colimits of arrows and transfinite composition.
- **claim-lifting-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lifting-test)): If $f\in\mathcal W$ has the right lifting property against $J_j$, it has the right lifting property against $I_j$. Moreover $$I\text{-}\mathrm{inj} =\mathcal W\cap J\text{-}\mathrm{inj}.$$ Every model maps to the terminal model with the right lifting property against all $J_j$.
  - Explanation: A weak equivalence lifting against the source inclusions also lifts against the boundary inclusions, giving a description of the trivial fibrations.
- **claim-cutoff-reflector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff)): The inclusion $\mathcal A_q\subseteq\mathcal A$ has a left adjoint $R_q$. Its unit $X\longrightarrow R_qX$ is bijective on the sets of cells in every dimension at most $q$. For $0\leq k<q$, the same construction gives the reflector $\mathcal A_q\to\mathcal A_k$, and $R_kR_q$ is naturally isomorphic to $R_k$.
  - Explanation: Models with unique fillers above a cutoff dimension form a reflective subcategory whose reflection keeps all cells through the cutoff.
- **claim-high-cells-preserve-low** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-high-cells)): Fix $k\geq0$. In $\mathcal A$, any relative cellular extension using only $I_j$ with $j>k$ is bijective on all cells through dimension $k$. The same holds in $\mathcal A_q$ whenever $k<q$. Consequently these conclusions also hold for relative extensions using only $J_j$ with $j>k$.
  - Explanation: Cellular extensions built only from boundary inclusions above a given dimension do not change the cells in lower dimensions, also after the cutoff.
- **claim-finite-spine** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spine)): For every finite globular-sum shape $S$ and every $m\geq0$, the subshape $T_m(S)$ has height at most $m+1$ and contains every cell of $S$ through dimension $m$. The inclusion $T_m(S)\to S$ is a finite composite of pushouts of the globular source inclusions $d_j\to d_{j+1}$ with $j>m$.
  - Explanation: Every finite globular sum shape has a low-height subshape containing all its cells up to a given dimension, reachable by source inclusions of larger index.
- **claim-shape-contractible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-shape-contractible)): For every nonempty finite globular-sum shape $S$, the models $F(S)$ and $F_q(S)$ are contractible, for every $q\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Free models on nonempty finite globular sum shapes, and their cutoff versions, are contractible.
- **claim-top-base-case** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-base)): The assertion $\mathsf E(q,q)$ holds for every $q\geq0$.
  - Explanation: The top-dimensional case of the elementary expansion assertion at a fixed cutoff holds by the unit retraction and unique higher fillers.
- **claim-upper-cell-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-upper-cell)): Suppose $i:U\to V$ is an upper $I$-cellular extension, $U$ is cellular, and $i\in\mathcal W$. Then $i$ admits a retraction. For every map $U\to U'$ with $U'$ cellular, the pushout $$U'\longrightarrow U'\amalg_U V$$ also belongs to $\mathcal W$.
  - Explanation: An upper cellular weak equivalence out of a cellular object splits, and its pushouts along maps to cellular objects remain weak equivalences.
- **claim-tree-contractible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tree-contractible)): Under the fixed-cutoff induction hypotheses above, $F_q(K)$ is contractible for every tree diagram $K$ at depth $n$.
  - Explanation: Under the induction hypotheses for higher indices, free models on tree diagrams at the given depth are contractible; this is shown by comparing the tree with a linear string.
- **claim-whiskered-shape-contractible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-whiskered-shape)): Under the fixed-cutoff induction hypotheses above, $H_\epsilon(S)$ is contractible for both choices of $\epsilon$. The same conclusion holds if the orientation of the join is chosen independently for each $n$-cell of $S$.
  - Explanation: Adjoining a duplicate and a joining cell at each cell of the chosen dimension to a free shape keeps it contractible, for either or mixed orientations.
- **claim-cylinder-skeletal-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder-skeletal-gluing)): Fix $\epsilon\in\{0,1\}$. For a finite globular set $S$, define, whenever the required cylinder objects have been constructed, $$\label{eq:cylinder-skeletal-stage} C_\ell(S)=F(S)\amalg_{F(\mathop{\mathrm{sk}}_\ell S),e_\epsilon} P(\mathop{\mathrm{sk}}_\ell S).$$ For $\ell>n$, there is a pushout square $$\label{eq:cylinder-skeletal-pushout} ```tex \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center), node distance=1.35cm and 2.5cm,>=Stealth] \node (a) {\(\displaystyle\coprod_{s\in S_\ell} A_\ell^\epsilon\)}; \node (b) [right=of a] {\(C_{\ell-1}(S)\)}; \node (c) [below=of a] {\(\displaystyle\coprod_{s\in S_\ell} P_\ell\)}; \node (d) at (b \|- c) {\(C_\ell(S)\).}; \draw[->] (a) -- (b); \draw[->] (a) -- (c); \draw[->] (b) -- (d); \draw[->] (c) -- (d); \end{tikzpicture} ```$$ Here $S_\ell$ is the set of actual $\ell$-cells of the globular set $S$. If $S$ is a pasting scheme, then $$\label{eq:cylinder-skeletal-ends} C_n(S)=H_\epsilon(S),\qquad C_{j-1}(d_j)=A_j^\epsilon\quad(j>n),\qquad C_{\dim(S)}(S)=P(S).$$ The first identity uses the whisker orientation for which the retained copy of $S$ is endpoint $\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: The cylinder construction on a finite globular set is assembled skeleton by skeleton through pushouts of one-sided cylinder domains, with the stated identifications at the ends.
- **claim-cylinder-contractible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cylinder-contractibility)): Every $P_j$ is contractible. For $n<j\le q$, both one-sided domains $A_j^\epsilon$ are contractible, and $A_j^\epsilon\to P_j$ is an upper cellular weak equivalence. For every pasting scheme $S$ of height at most $q$, the model $P(S)$ is cellular and contractible.
  - Explanation: All cylinder objects and their one-sided domains are contractible, the comparison between them is an upper cellular weak equivalence, and the realization on pasting schemes is cellular and contractible.
- **claim-partial-path-functor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-partial-path)): The construction in Equation <a href="#eq:partial-path-cells" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:partial-path-cells">[eq:partial-path-cells]</a> admits interpretations of every operation of $\mathcal C$, making $\mathcal P:\mathcal A_q\to\mathcal A_q$ a functor and both $p_\epsilon$ natural model morphisms. They lift all boundary inclusions $I_j$. Their joint map $$(p_0,p_1):\mathcal P Y\longrightarrow Y\times Y$$ lifts $J_n$.
  - Explanation: The partial path construction interprets every operation of the coherator, is functorial, has natural endpoint maps that lift the boundary inclusions, and its joint endpoint map lifts the relevant source inclusion.
- **claim-semi-model-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-semimodel)): For every Grothendieck coherator $\mathcal C$, the category $\mathcal A$ admits a cofibrantly generated left semi-model structure with $$\begin{aligned} \text{weak equivalences}&=\mathcal W,\\ \text{cofibrations}&=I\text{-}\mathrm{cof},\\ \text{fibrations}&=J\text{-}\mathrm{inj},\\ \text{trivial fibrations}&=I\text{-}\mathrm{inj}. \end{aligned}$$ Here $\mathcal W$ is the class defined by components and based homotopy groups. Cofibrant objects are exactly retracts of cellular objects, and every object is fibrant.
  - Explanation: For each coherator the category of models carries a cofibrantly generated left semi-model structure whose weak equivalences are the maps inducing isomorphisms on components and homotopy groups.
- **claim-homotopy-hypothesis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-homotopy-hypothesis)): For every Grothendieck coherator $\mathcal C$, the canonical semi-model category $\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}(\mathcal C)$ is Quillen equivalent to spaces. More precisely, there is a geometric-realization/fundamental- infinity-groupoid adjunction $$\|-\|:\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}(\mathcal C)\ \rightleftarrows\ \mathbf{Spaces}:\Pi_\infty$$ which is a Quillen equivalence. In particular, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}(\mathcal C)[\mathcal W^{-1}] \simeq \operatorname{Ho}(\mathbf{Spaces}).$$ Thus the Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis holds for these groupoids.
  - Explanation: The canonical semi-model category of models of any coherator is Quillen equivalent to spaces, which is the Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-weak-test",
        "exact_quote": "For a model map $f:X\\to Y$, the following are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *$f\\in\\mathcal W$.*\n\n2.  *It induces a bijection on $\\pi_0$ and a bijection $$\\pi_r(X;u,v)\\longrightarrow\\pi_r(Y;fu,fv)$$ for every $r\\ge1$ and every parallel pair $u,v$ in $X$.*\n\n3.  *For every $r\\ge0$, every specified boundary $b:\\partial D_r\\to X$, and every $r$-cell $y$ of $Y$ with boundary $fb$, there are an $r$-cell $x$ with boundary *exactly* $b$ and an $(r+1)$-cell $fx\\to y$ in $Y$.*\n\n*In particular, a weak equivalence reflects homotopy between parallel cells, including equality of components for vertices.",

      },
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **globular theory** ([source](#stmt-def-coherator)): A *globular theory* is a category under $\Theta_0$, with the same objects and preserving the designated globular sums.
- **Grothendieck coherator** ([source](#stmt-def-coherator)): A *Grothendieck coherator* $\mathcal C$ is such a theory with the following two properties.
- **contractible model** ([source](#stmt-def-prelim-contractible)): A model $X$ is *contractible* if $X\to1$ belongs to $\mathcal W$.
- **weak equivalences W** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Let $\mathcal W$ consist of the maps inducing a bijection on $\pi_0$ and isomorphisms on all $\pi_r$ at all source vertices.
- **cellular extension and cellular object** ([source](#pm-h-5)): An *$I$-cellular extension* is a transfinite composite of pushouts of coproducts of the $I_j$; an object is *cellular* if its map from the initial model is such an extension.
- **homotopy relation between cells** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $\alpha\sim\beta$ when there is one $(r+1)$-cell from an $r$-cell $\alpha$ to a parallel $r$-cell $\beta$.
- **q-coskeletal model** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For an integer $q\geq0$, call a model *$q$-coskeletal* if every globular boundary of dimension $j>q$ has exactly one filler.
- **expansion assertion E(q,n)** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For $0\leq n\leq q$, let $\mathsf E(q,n)$ be the following assertion:
- **tree diagram at depth n** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Define a *tree diagram at depth $n$* as follows.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-homotopy-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-prelim-division) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prelim-division): Build universal correction expressions by induction on dimension and show multiplication maps have two-sided inverses on homotopy classes. -> division bijection with prescribed boundaries
- **step-boundary-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-prelim-boundary-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prelim-boundary-change): Compare endomorphism sets at different boundaries through spans of division bijections. -> identification with based homotopy groups
- **step-weak-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-weak-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-weak-test): Prove the three conditions equivalent by induction on dimension. -> exact-boundary criterion for weak equivalence
- **step-weak-closure** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weak-closure): Use components and based groups for two-out-of-three, the exact-boundary test for retracts and filtered colimits. -> closure properties of weak equivalences
- **step-lifting-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-lifting-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lifting-test): Correct an approximate lift by lifting its homotopy against the source inclusion. -> description of trivial fibrations
- **step-cutoff-reflector** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff): Add unique fillers above the cutoff and interpret the free generators in their original order. -> reflector preserving cells through the cutoff
- **step-high-cells** [claim](#stmt-lem-high-cells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-high-cells): Reflect to a lower cutoff where high attachments are isomorphisms. -> high attachments preserve low cells
- **step-spine** [claim](#stmt-lem-spine) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spine): Recursive description of shapes by suspension, with the spine built by source inclusions. -> finite spine of bounded height
- **step-shape-contractible** [claim](#stmt-prop-shape-contractible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-shape-contractible): Lift parallel cells to the spine and use an admissible filler of the coherator. -> contractibility of free shapes
- **step-top-case** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-base): Use the unit retraction and unique fillers above the cutoff to check the exact-boundary criterion. -> top-dimensional case
- **step-upper-cell** [claim](#stmt-lem-upper-cell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-upper-cell): Factor through upper source cellular maps, obtain a retraction and push out the retract diagram. -> splitting and stable pushouts of upper cellular weak equivalences
- **step-tree-contractible** [claim](#stmt-prop-tree-contractible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tree-contractible): Compare the tree with a linear string, promote generator homotopies using higher expansions and conclude by two-out-of-three. -> contractible tree diagram models
- **step-whiskers** [claim](#stmt-prop-whiskered-shape) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-whiskered-shape): Reduce to the spine, which gives a tree diagram, then pass to the whole shape by upper cellular weak equivalences. -> contractible whiskered shapes
- **step-skeletal-gluing** [claim](#stmt-lem-cylinder-skeletal-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cylinder-skeletal-gluing): Use that skeleta are built by attaching globes and that both free and cylinder constructions preserve these colimits. -> skeletal pushout formula
- **step-cylinder-contractible** [claim](#stmt-prop-cylinder-contractibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cylinder-contractibility): Induct on the cylinder dimension, filtering through the skeletal pushouts and using stability of upper cellular weak equivalences. -> contractible cylinders and pasting realizations
- **step-partial-path** [claim](#stmt-prop-partial-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-partial-path): Interpret generators of the coherator in their free order using Yoneda and contractibility to extend prescribed data, then verify individual and joint lifting. -> path functor with lifting endpoints
- **step-descending-induction** [claim](#stmt-prop-partial-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-partial-path-4): Build a section and a lift against the joint endpoint map, then show the expansion is a retract-related weak equivalence. -> expansion assertion at a fixed cutoff
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-partial-path-5): Reflect to a sufficiently large cutoff and transfer the exact-boundary test back to the uncut category. -> elementary expansion theorem
- **step-semi-model** [claim](#stmt-cor-semimodel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-semimodel): Extend the expansion theorem to cofibrant starts by retracts, then verify factorizations and liftings with the small object argument. -> left semi-model structure
- **step-homotopy-hypothesis** [claim](#stmt-cor-homotopy-hypothesis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-homotopy-hypothesis): Verify the cylinder coherator hypotheses of Henry's comparison for finite cellular models and apply his recognition result. -> Quillen equivalence with spaces

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-elementary-expansion-weak-equivalence**: For any Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention, attaching a higher disk along its source face to a cellular model, by any attaching cell, gives a weak equivalence.
- **inv-exact-boundary-weak-test**: A model map is a weak equivalence exactly when it induces a bijection on components and on homotopy sets between parallel cells, or equivalently when it satisfies the exact-boundary filling test.
- **inv-homotopy-hypothesis**: For each Grothendieck coherator in the convention used, its category of models with the canonical semi-model structure is Quillen equivalent to spaces.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-strict-groupoids**: Applying the expansion and homotopy-hypothesis conclusions to strict globular groupoids. -> Strict groupoids represent only a restricted class of homotopy types, so the weak-coherence argument does not transfer.
- **anti-noncellular-start**: Claiming that adjoining a cell to an arbitrary non-cellular model is a weak equivalence. -> The proof uses a cellular presentation of the starting model; without it the argument is not available.
- **anti-full-model-category**: Treating the category of models as a full two-sided model category. -> Only a left semi-model structure is established, so factorizations and lifts are restricted to cofibrant domains.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existential homotopy-theoretic statements about weak equivalences, semi-model structures and Quillen equivalences for arbitrary, possibly transfinite, cellular models. The proofs use transfinite induction, small-object arguments and retract closure, with no finite procedure whose output could be computed and checked on test inputs.

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result is an existence statement about weak equivalences in models of an abstract coherator. It has no input-to-output procedure to implement, and the proofs use transfinite and small-object constructions that no program can run. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no dynamics, state evolution or sampling that could be simulated. The cylinder and path objects are abstract presheaf models, not simulation primitives. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a proof about homotopy theory and ships no checker, converter or analyser. Models of a coherator are generally infinite, so no tool can enumerate their cells to test the weak-equivalence criterion. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No algorithm with a termination or complexity claim is stated. The constructions use choices of fillers and transfinite composites, so they are existence arguments and carry no pseudocode or cost bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Pasting schemes and tree diagrams are finite combinatorial shapes. They serve only as arities of operations and are not an indexing or storage structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no numerical kernel, dataflow or bounded-precision computation to map onto hardware. The objects are categorical and infinite in general. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no hardness assumptions, adversaries or security notions. Nothing in the homotopy comparison yields a primitive or a reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning objective, data distribution or generalisation statement. The Quillen equivalence with spaces is a statement of pure homotopy theory. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or bound on an optimum appears. The closure properties of the weak-equivalence class are structural, not extremal. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages, faults or consistency guarantees. Pasting diagrams and trees are not communication graphs, and path inversion has no causal meaning. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The contribution is a theoretical result on homotopy theory, relevant to higher category theory and as a possible target for formal verification. It gives no concrete construction in any other engineering domain. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-cellular-start** (hypothesis): The main expansion theorem is stated only for a cellular starting model and a cell from a disk into it, over one fixed Grothendieck coherator. Nothing is claimed for arbitrary non-cellular models. Blocked use: ['Concluding that adjoining an (n+1)-cell to an arbitrary non-cellular model is a weak equivalence', 'Applying the theorem to a presheaf whose map from the initial model is not an I-cellular extension']
- **limit-arity-height-convention** (hypothesis): The proof uses the convention that generating fillers have arity of height at most their output dimension. Composite operations may have arities of larger height, and the argument avoids any bound on them. Coherators outside this convention are not covered. Blocked use: ['Transferring the result to globular theories whose generating fillers may have arities of height above the output dimension', 'Replacing the chosen free cellular order of operations by an order on output dimensions']
- **limit-fixed-cutoff-induction** (edge_case): The core expansion argument is a descending induction at a fixed cutoff, where all higher indices are assumed already known. The tree, whisker and cylinder results hold only under those induction hypotheses, not as unconditional statements. Blocked use: ['Quoting tree or whiskered-shape contractibility without the higher-index expansion assumption', 'Using the partial path functor outside the cutoff category and index range for which it is built']
- **limit-transfinite-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): Cellular objects may have arbitrary set-sized transfinite presentations, and the proofs use small-object constructions, choices of fillers and retract closure. These are existence arguments with no finite procedure or complexity bound. Blocked use: ['Reading the proofs as an algorithm that decides or computes weak equivalence of cellular models', 'Giving running-time or storage claims for the construction of the cylinder objects or the path functor']
- **limit-left-semi-model** (formal_scope): Only a left semi-model structure is established. Factorization and lifting of trivial cofibrations against fibrations are required only for maps with cofibrant domain, so full two-sided model-category behaviour is not claimed. Blocked use: ['Using trivial-cofibration factorization on maps with non-cofibrant domain', 'Assuming pushouts of trivial cofibrations preserve weak equivalences on all objects']
- **limit-weak-equivalence-meaning** (formal_scope): Weak equivalences are defined inside the chosen globular algebraic category by components and based homotopy groups. The comparison with spaces rests on the cited cylinder-category recognition result and the expansion theorem together. Blocked use: ['Reading the Quillen equivalence as independent of the cited comparison theory', 'Applying the homotopy-hypothesis conclusion to a different notion of weak equivalence']

```json
          "anchor": "sec:introduction",

        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-21",
          "exact_quote": "#### The comparison with spaces We use the cylinder-category comparison established in (Henry 2016, sec. 5.1 and 5.3) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. The relevant implication is all-dimensional and has exactly the expansion assertion as its additional hypothesis. We spell out its application to the fixed coherator. The comparison first packages finite cellular models and their cylinders into a small category. Its completion recovers all models of the same coherator. Henry’s recognition theorem then identifies that completion with spaces up to Quillen equivalence. The proof below records the hypotheses at each of these three interfaces. A cylinder category has cofibrations and weak equivalences satisfying (Henry 2016, Definition 2.1.1) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]; in particular, it has cofibration pushouts, its weak equivalences satisfy two-out-of-six, every object has a cylinder, and every trivial cofibration retracts. Its completion is the full category of set-valued presheaves taking the initial object to a singleton and cofibration pushouts to pullbacks (Henry 2016, Definition 2.4.1) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. Here $\\mathbf{Spaces}$ denotes the usual model category of topological spaces, with weak homotopy equivalences. <div id=\"cor:homotopy-hypothesis\" class=\"corollary\"> **Corollary 21**. *For every Grothendieck coherator $\\mathcal C$, the canonical semi-model category $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}(\\mathcal C)$ is Quillen equivalent to spaces. More precisely, there is a geometric-realization/fundamental- infinity-groupoid adjunction $$|-|:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}(\\mathcal C)\\ \\rightleftarrows\\ \\mathbf{Spaces}:\\Pi_\\infty$$ which is a Quillen equivalence. In particular, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}(\\mathcal C)[\\mathcal W^{-1}] \\simeq \\operatorname{Ho}(\\mathbf{Spaces}).$$ Thus the Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis holds for these groupoids.* </div> <details id=\"proof-stmt-cor-homotopy-hypothesis\"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-homotopy-hypothesis</summary> *Proof.* Let $\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ be a small skeleton of the full subcategory of finitely cellular $\\mathcal C$-groupoids, with finite boundary-cell extensions as cofibrations and the restriction of $\\mathcal W$ as weak equivalences. Henry’s comparison uses the following three facts. First, under the expansion assertion, $\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ is a cylinder category (Henry 2016, Proposition 5.3.6) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. The finite nature of its cylinders is explicit: a relative cylinder for the generating cofibration $\\partial D_j\\to D_j$ is $$D_j\\amalg_{\\partial D_j}D_j \\longrightarrow D_{j+1}\\longrightarrow D_j.$$ The first map is $I_{j+1}$, and the source endpoint is $J_j$; the last map is induced by a unit. The source endpoint is a weak equivalence by Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, and hence so is the last map. Finite relative cylinders are obtained by the finite cell induction of that proposition. Trivial cofibrations in this category have retractions by Corollary <a href=\"#cor:semimodel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:semimodel\">20</a>, since their domains are cofibrant and all objects are fibrant. Second, the free construction of the original coherator identifies the completion of $\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}(\\mathcal C)$, equipped with precisely the structure in Corollary <a href=\"#cor:semimodel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:semimodel\">20</a> (Henry 2016, Proposition 5.3.7). In this construction one begins with finite globular sets and adjoins the operations in the given free order of $\\mathcal C$. Thus the identification retains the chosen theory and its actual algebraic models. The generating cofibrations in the completion are the $I_j$, and its generating trivial cofibrations are the relative-cylinder endpoints $J_j$. Third, the marked object $D_0$ makes $\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ a cylinder coherator (Henry 2016, Corollary 5.3.12) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. In Henry’s terminology this means that the map from the free pre-cylinder category $F_*$ on one object, selecting $D_0$, is a trivial cofibration and that the target is a cylinder category (Henry 2016, Definition 5.1.1) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. The hypotheses used in this implication are the free cellular construction of $\\mathcal C$, the expansion assertion, and the homotopical terminality of $D_0$. The first is part of Definition <a href=\"#def:coherator\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:coherator\">2</a>, the second is Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, and the third follows here also from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:shape-contractible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:shape-contractible\">12</a>: the map $D_0\\to1$ is $I$-injective by the boundary-filling characterization of contractibility. Every map $A\\to D_0$ therefore extends across every finite cellular cofibration $A\\to B$; this gives homotopical terminality inside $\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$. More explicitly, the projection $p:\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}^{D_0}\\to\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ in Henry’s construction is an acyclic fibration by (Henry 2016, Proposition 4.4.4) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)].[^2] The relative cofibrancy of $F_*\\to\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{fin}}$ from (Henry 2016, Proposition 5.3.7) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)] therefore gives a section of $p$ sending the marked $D_0$ to its cylinder $(D_0,D_1)$. The lifting argument of (Henry 2016, Lemma 5.3.9) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)] then gives the asserted cylinder-coherator property, as in (Henry 2016, Lemma 5.3.11 and Corollary 5.3.12) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. Henry’s Theorem 5.1.2(5) now gives a fundamental-infinity-groupoid right Quillen equivalence from spaces to this completion, with geometric realization as its left adjoint. By the second step the completion is the original $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}(\\mathcal C)$ with its canonical weak equivalences. This proves the displayed statements and is the implication summarized in (Henry 2016, Corollary 5.3.13) [[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. ◻ </details>",
          "source_id": "PM-312.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-weak-equivalence-meaning",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "Weak equivalences are defined inside the chosen globular algebraic category by components and based homotopy groups. The comparison with spaces rests on the cited cylinder-category recognition result and the expansion theorem together.",
      "support": "stated"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-312.1",
  "schema": "pm.limits.v2"
}
```

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 312 (`lean/docs/312.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis via elementary expansions](../../preprints/The-Grothendieck-homotopy-hypothesis-via-elementary-expansions-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis asks whether algebraic $\infty$-groupoids recover the homotopy theory of spaces. The formalized result is the elementary-expansion theorem used in this approach: for every Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention and every boundary-cellular model, attaching an $(n+1)$-disk along its source $n$-face induces a weak equivalence.
>
> The later semi-model structure and full comparison with the homotopy theory of spaces are not included.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

universe u v w

namespace Grothendieck

structure GlobularSet where
  Cell : ℕ → Type u
  source : ∀ n, Cell (n + 1) → Cell n
  target : ∀ n, Cell (n + 1) → Cell n
  source_source : ∀ n x, source n (source (n + 1) x) = source n (target (n + 1) x)
  target_source : ∀ n x, target n (source (n + 1) x) = target n (target (n + 1) x)

namespace GlobularSet

@[ext]
structure Hom (X Y : GlobularSet.{u}) where
  app : ∀ n, X.Cell n → Y.Cell n
  source_app : ∀ n x, app n (X.source n x) = Y.source n (app (n + 1) x)
  target_app : ∀ n x, app n (X.target n x) = Y.target n (app (n + 1) x)

def Parallel (X : GlobularSet.{u}) : ∀ n, X.Cell n → X.Cell n → Prop
  | 0, _, _ => True
  | n + 1, x, y => X.source n x = X.source n y ∧ X.target n x = X.target n y

def Joined (X : GlobularSet.{u}) (n : ℕ) (x y : X.Cell n) : Prop :=
  ∃ h : X.Cell (n + 1), X.source n h = x ∧ X.target n h = y

def Boundary (X : GlobularSet.{u}) : ℕ → Type u
  | 0 => PUnit
  | n + 1 => { p : X.Cell n × X.Cell n // X.Parallel n p.1 p.2 }

def boundary (X : GlobularSet.{u}) : ∀ n, X.Cell n → X.Boundary n
  | 0, _ => PUnit.unit
  | n + 1, x => ⟨(X.source n x, X.target n x), by
      cases n with
      | zero => trivial
      | succ n => exact ⟨X.source_source n x, X.target_source n x⟩⟩

def Hom.boundaryMap {X Y : GlobularSet.{u}} (f : Hom X Y) : ∀ n, X.Boundary n → Y.Boundary n
  | 0, _ => PUnit.unit
  | n + 1, b => ⟨(f.app n b.1.1, f.app n b.1.2), by
      cases n with
      | zero => trivial
      | succ n =>
        exact ⟨by rw [← f.source_app, ← f.source_app, b.2.1],
          by rw [← f.target_app, ← f.target_app, b.2.2]⟩⟩

end GlobularSet

open CategoryTheory CategoryTheory.Limits Opposite

@[ext]
structure Globe where
  dim : ℕ

namespace Globe

inductive Face : ℕ → ℕ → Type
  | id (n : ℕ) : Face n n
  | face {m n : ℕ} (lt : m < n) (side : Bool) : Face m n

namespace Face

def comp {m n k : ℕ} : Face m n → Face n k → Face m k
  | .id _, g => g
  | .face h b, .id _ => .face h b
  | .face h b, .face h' _ => .face (h.trans h') b

end Face

instance : Category Globe where
  Hom m n := Face m.dim n.dim
  id m := .id m.dim
  comp := Face.comp
  id_comp _ := rfl
  comp_id {X Y} f := by cases X; cases Y; cases f <;> rfl
  assoc {W X Y Z} f g h := by
    cases W; cases X; cases Y; cases Z
    cases f <;> cases g <;> cases h <;> rfl

def face {m n : ℕ} (h : m < n) (side : Bool) : Globe.mk m ⟶ Globe.mk n :=
  Face.face h side

def source (n : ℕ) : Globe.mk n ⟶ Globe.mk (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) false

def target (n : ℕ) : Globe.mk n ⟶ Globe.mk (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) true

end Globe

abbrev GlobularPresheaf := Globeᵒᵖ ⥤ Type u

namespace GlobularPresheaf

def underlying (X : GlobularPresheaf.{u}) : GlobularSet.{u} where
  Cell n := X.obj (op (Globe.mk n))
  source n := X.map (Globe.source n).op
  target n := X.map (Globe.target n).op
  source_source n x := by
    have h := congrArg (fun f => X.map f) (congrArg Quiver.Hom.op (show Globe.source n ≫ Globe.source (n + 1) = Globe.source n ≫ Globe.target (n + 1) from rfl))
    simpa only [op_comp, X.map_comp, types_comp_apply] using ConcreteCategory.congr_hom h x
  target_source n x := by
    have h := congrArg (fun f => X.map f) (congrArg Quiver.Hom.op (show Globe.target n ≫ Globe.source (n + 1) = Globe.target n ≫ Globe.target (n + 1) from rfl))
    simpa only [op_comp, X.map_comp, types_comp_apply] using ConcreteCategory.congr_hom h x

def underlyingMap {X Y : GlobularPresheaf.{u}} (f : X ⟶ Y) : X.underlying.Hom Y.underlying where
  app n := f.app (op (Globe.mk n))
  source_app n x := by
    exact ConcreteCategory.congr_hom (f.naturality (Globe.source n).op) x
  target_app n x := by
    exact ConcreteCategory.congr_hom (f.naturality (Globe.target n).op) x

def disk (n : ℕ) : GlobularPresheaf.{u} := uliftYoneda.{u}.obj (Globe.mk n)

def face {m n : ℕ} (h : m < n) (side : Bool) : disk.{u} m ⟶ disk.{u} n :=
  uliftYoneda.{u}.map (Globe.face h side)

end GlobularPresheaf

inductive Table : ℕ → Type
  | globe (n : ℕ) : Table n
  | attach {last : ℕ} (T : Table last) (b h : ℕ) (left : b < last) (right : b < h) : Table h

namespace Table

def height : {n : ℕ} → Table n → ℕ
  | _, .globe n => n
  | _, .attach T _ h _ _ => max T.height h

noncomputable def realizeWithLast : {n : ℕ} → Table n →
    (Σ X : GlobularPresheaf.{0}, GlobularPresheaf.disk n ⟶ X)
  | _, .globe n => ⟨GlobularPresheaf.disk n, 𝟙 _⟩
  | _, .attach T _ h hl hr =>
    let prev := T.realizeWithLast
    let f := GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ prev.2
    let g := GlobularPresheaf.face hr false
    ⟨pushout f g, pushout.inr f g⟩

noncomputable def realize {n} (T : Table n) : GlobularPresheaf.{0} := T.realizeWithLast.1

noncomputable def lastInclusion {n} (T : Table n) : GlobularPresheaf.disk n ⟶ T.realize :=
  T.realizeWithLast.2

end Table

abbrev Shape := Σ n, Table n

namespace Shape

noncomputable def realize (S : Shape) : GlobularPresheaf.{0} := S.2.realize

def height (S : Shape) : ℕ := S.2.height

def globe (n : ℕ) : Shape := ⟨n, Table.globe n⟩

noncomputable instance : Category Shape where
  Hom S T := S.realize ⟶ T.realize
  id S := 𝟙 S.realize
  comp f g := f ≫ g
  id_comp := Category.id_comp
  comp_id := Category.comp_id
  assoc := Category.assoc

noncomputable def face {m n : ℕ} (h : m < n) (side : Bool) : globe m ⟶ globe n :=
  GlobularPresheaf.face h side

noncomputable def source (n : ℕ) : globe n ⟶ globe (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) false

noncomputable def target (n : ℕ) : globe n ⟶ globe (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) true

def attach (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) : Shape :=
  ⟨h, Table.attach S.2 b h hl hr⟩

noncomputable def seam (S : Shape) {b : ℕ} (hl : b < S.1) : globe b ⟶ S :=
  show GlobularPresheaf.disk b ⟶ S.realize from
    GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion

noncomputable def inl (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) :
    S ⟶ S.attach b h hl hr :=
  pushout.inl (GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion)
    (GlobularPresheaf.face hr false)

noncomputable def inr (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) :
    globe h ⟶ S.attach b h hl hr :=
  pushout.inr (GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion)
    (GlobularPresheaf.face hr false)

end Shape

structure TheoryData where
  Hom : Shape → Shape → Type v
  id : ∀ S, Hom S S
  comp : ∀ {S T U}, Hom S T → Hom T U → Hom S U
  id_comp : ∀ {S T} (f : Hom S T), comp (id S) f = f
  comp_id : ∀ {S T} (f : Hom S T), comp f (id T) = f
  assoc : ∀ {R S T U} (f : Hom R S) (g : Hom S T) (h : Hom T U),
    comp (comp f g) h = comp f (comp g h)
  base : ∀ {S T}, (S ⟶ T) → Hom S T
  base_id : ∀ S, base (𝟙 S) = id S
  base_comp : ∀ {R S T} (f : R ⟶ S) (g : S ⟶ T), base (f ≫ g) = comp (base f) (base g)

namespace TheoryData

structure Obj (C : TheoryData.{v}) where
  shape : Shape

instance (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Category C.Obj where
  Hom S T := C.Hom S.shape T.shape
  id S := C.id S.shape
  comp f g := C.comp f g
  id_comp := C.id_comp
  comp_id := C.comp_id
  assoc := C.assoc

noncomputable def arity (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Shape ⥤ C.Obj where
  obj S := ⟨S⟩
  map f := C.base f
  map_id := C.base_id
  map_comp := C.base_comp

def PreservesGlobularSums (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Prop :=
  ∀ (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h),
    IsPushout (C.arity.map (S.seam hl)) (C.arity.map (Shape.face hr false))
      (C.arity.map (S.inl b h hl hr)) (C.arity.map (S.inr b h hl hr))

end TheoryData

structure GlobularTheory extends TheoryData.{v} where
  sums : toTheoryData.PreservesGlobularSums

namespace GlobularTheory

abbrev Obj (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) := C.toTheoryData.Obj

@[ext]
structure Morphism (C D : GlobularTheory.{v}) where
  map : ∀ {S T}, C.Hom S T → D.Hom S T
  map_id : ∀ S, map (C.id S) = D.id S
  map_comp : ∀ {R S T} (f : C.Hom R S) (g : C.Hom S T),
    map (C.comp f g) = D.comp (map f) (map g)
  map_base : ∀ {S T} (f : S ⟶ T), map (C.base f) = D.base f

namespace Morphism

def id (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Morphism C C where
  map f := f
  map_id _ := rfl
  map_comp _ _ := rfl
  map_base _ := rfl

def comp {C D E : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D) (g : Morphism D E) : Morphism C E where
  map a := g.map (f.map a)
  map_id S := by rw [f.map_id, g.map_id]
  map_comp a b := by rw [f.map_comp, g.map_comp]
  map_base a := by rw [f.map_base, g.map_base]

end Morphism

def Parallel (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) {S : Shape} :
    ∀ n, C.Hom (Shape.globe n) S → C.Hom (Shape.globe n) S → Prop
  | 0, _, _ => True
  | n + 1, f, g =>
    C.comp (C.base (Shape.source n)) f = C.comp (C.base (Shape.source n)) g ∧
    C.comp (C.base (Shape.target n)) f = C.comp (C.base (Shape.target n)) g

structure AdmissiblePair (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where
  n : ℕ
  arity : Shape
  height : arity.height ≤ n + 1
  source : C.Hom (Shape.globe n) arity
  target : C.Hom (Shape.globe n) arity
  parallel : C.Parallel n source target

def Fills {C D : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D) (p : C.AdmissiblePair)
    (z : D.Hom (Shape.globe (p.n + 1)) p.arity) : Prop :=
  D.comp (D.base (Shape.source p.n)) z = f.map p.source ∧
  D.comp (D.base (Shape.target p.n)) z = f.map p.target

def IsFreeExtension {C D : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D)
    (G : Type v) (pair : G → C.AdmissiblePair)
    (filler : ∀ g, D.Hom (Shape.globe ((pair g).n + 1)) (pair g).arity) : Prop :=
  (∀ g, Fills f (pair g) (filler g)) ∧
  ∀ (E : GlobularTheory.{v}) (a : Morphism C E)
    (chosen : ∀ g, E.Hom (Shape.globe ((pair g).n + 1)) (pair g).arity),
    (∀ g, Fills a (pair g) (chosen g)) →
    ∃! b : Morphism D E, f.comp b = a ∧ ∀ g, b.map (filler g) = chosen g

structure CellularPresentation (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where
  stage : ℕ → GlobularTheory.{v}
  initial : ∀ D : GlobularTheory.{v}, Nonempty (Unique (Morphism (stage 0) D))
  step : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) (stage (r + 1))
  generators : ℕ → Type v
  pair : ∀ r, generators r → (stage r).AdmissiblePair
  filler : ∀ r g, (stage (r + 1)).Hom (Shape.globe ((pair r g).n + 1)) (pair r g).arity
  free : ∀ r, IsFreeExtension (step r) (generators r) (pair r) (filler r)
  inclusion : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) C
  compatible : ∀ r, (step r).comp (inclusion (r + 1)) = inclusion r
  colimit : ∀ (D : GlobularTheory.{v}) (a : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) D),
    (∀ r, (step r).comp (a (r + 1)) = a r) →
    ∃! b : Morphism C D, ∀ r, (inclusion r).comp b = a r

def IsCoherator (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Prop :=
  Nonempty C.CellularPresentation ∧
  ∀ p : C.AdmissiblePair, ∃ z, Fills (Morphism.id C) p z

noncomputable def shapeGlobes : Globe ⥤ Shape where
  obj n := Shape.globe n.dim
  map f := uliftYoneda.map f
  map_id n := by exact uliftYoneda.map_id n
  map_comp f g := by exact uliftYoneda.map_comp f g

noncomputable def globes (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Globe ⥤ C.Obj :=
  shapeGlobes ⋙ C.toTheoryData.arity

structure Model (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where
  presheaf : C.Objᵒᵖ ⥤ Type v
  sums : ∀ (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h),
    IsPullback
      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.inl b h hl hr)).op)
      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.inr b h hl hr)).op)
      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.seam hl)).op)
      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (Shape.face hr false)).op)

namespace Model

instance (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Category C.Model where
  Hom X Y := X.presheaf ⟶ Y.presheaf
  id X := 𝟙 X.presheaf
  comp f g := f ≫ g
  id_comp := Category.id_comp
  comp_id := Category.comp_id
  assoc := Category.assoc

noncomputable def underlying {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : GlobularSet.{v} :=
  GlobularPresheaf.underlying (C.globes.op ⋙ X.presheaf)

noncomputable def underlyingMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) :
    X.underlying.Hom Y.underlying :=
  GlobularPresheaf.underlyingMap (Functor.whiskerLeft C.globes.op f)

noncomputable def freeShape (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (S : Shape) : C.Model where
  presheaf := yoneda.obj ⟨S⟩
  sums T b h hl hr := ((C.sums T b h hl hr).op.map (yoneda.obj ⟨S⟩)).flip

noncomputable def disk (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : C.Model :=
  freeShape C (Shape.globe n)

noncomputable def J (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : disk C n ⟶ disk C (n + 1) :=
  show (disk C n).presheaf ⟶ (disk C (n + 1)).presheaf from
    yoneda.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (Shape.source n))

structure BoundaryAttachment {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) where
  index : Type v
  dimension : index → ℕ
  boundary : ∀ k, X.underlying.Boundary (dimension k)
  cell : ∀ k, Y.underlying.Cell (dimension k)
  cell_boundary : ∀ k, Y.underlying.boundary _ (cell k) =
    (underlyingMap f).boundaryMap _ (boundary k)
  universal : ∀ (Z : C.Model) (g : X ⟶ Z)
    (z : ∀ k, Z.underlying.Cell (dimension k)),
    (∀ k, Z.underlying.boundary _ (z k) = (underlyingMap g).boundaryMap _ (boundary k)) →
    ∃! h : Y ⟶ Z, f ≫ h = g ∧ ∀ k, (underlyingMap h).app _ (cell k) = z k

structure CellularPresentation {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) where
  order : Type v
  linearOrder : LinearOrder order
  orderBot : OrderBot order
  succOrder : SuccOrder order
  wellFoundedLT : WellFoundedLT order
  initial : C.Model
  isInitial : IsInitial initial
  start : initial ⟶ X
  chain : @TransfiniteCompositionOfShape _ _ order linearOrder orderBot
    _ _ start succOrder wellFoundedLT
  attachment : ∀ j, ¬ IsMax j → BoundaryAttachment
    (chain.F.map (homOfLE (Order.le_succ j)))

def IsCellular {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : Prop := Nonempty X.CellularPresentation

end Model

def unitPair (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : C.AdmissiblePair where
  n := n
  arity := Shape.globe n
  height := Nat.le_succ n
  source := C.id (Shape.globe n)
  target := C.id (Shape.globe n)
  parallel := by cases n <;> simp [Parallel]

noncomputable def unit (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (hC : C.IsCoherator) (n : ℕ) :
    C.Hom (Shape.globe (n + 1)) (Shape.globe n) := Classical.choose (hC.2 (C.unitPair n))

def Model.eval {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) {S T : Shape} (f : C.Hom S T) :
    X.presheaf.obj (op (⟨T⟩ : C.Obj)) → X.presheaf.obj (op (⟨S⟩ : C.Obj)) :=
  X.presheaf.map (show (⟨S⟩ : C.Obj) ⟶ ⟨T⟩ from f).op

noncomputable def Model.unitCell {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)
    (n : ℕ) (x : X.underlying.Cell n) : X.underlying.Cell (n + 1) :=
  X.eval (C.unit hC n) x

noncomputable def Model.baseCell {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)
    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) : ∀ n, X.underlying.Cell n
  | 0 => x
  | n + 1 => X.unitCell hC n (X.baseCell hC x n)

def Model.Loops {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)
    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) : Type v :=
  { z : X.underlying.Cell (n + 1) // X.underlying.source n z = X.baseCell hC x n ∧
      X.underlying.target n z = X.baseCell hC x n }

def Model.Components {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : Type v :=
  Quot (X.underlying.Joined 0)

def Model.HomotopyGroup {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)
    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) : Type v :=
  Quot (fun a b : X.Loops hC x n => X.underlying.Joined (n + 1) a.1 b.1)

noncomputable def Model.loopsMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y)
    (hC : C.IsCoherator) (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) :
    X.Loops hC x n → Y.Loops hC ((underlyingMap f).app 0 x) n := by
  have hb : ∀ k, (underlyingMap f).app k (X.baseCell hC x k) =
      Y.baseCell hC ((underlyingMap f).app 0 x) k := by
    intro k
    induction k with
    | zero => rfl
    | succ k ih =>
      have h := ConcreteCategory.congr_hom (f.naturality
        (show (op (⟨Shape.globe k⟩ : C.Obj)) ⟶ op ⟨Shape.globe (k+1)⟩ from
          Quiver.Hom.op (C.unit hC k))) (X.baseCell hC x k)
      change (underlyingMap f).app (k+1) (X.unitCell hC k (X.baseCell hC x k)) =
        Y.unitCell hC k ((underlyingMap f).app k (X.baseCell hC x k)) at h
      simpa only [baseCell, ih] using h
  exact fun z => ⟨(underlyingMap f).app (n + 1) z.1,
    by rw [← (underlyingMap f).source_app, z.2.1, hb],
    by rw [← (underlyingMap f).target_app, z.2.2, hb]⟩

noncomputable def Model.componentsMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) :
    X.Components → Y.Components :=
  Quot.map ((underlyingMap f).app 0) (by
    rintro _ _ ⟨h, hs, ht⟩
    exact ⟨(underlyingMap f).app _ h,
      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).source_app, hs],
      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).target_app, ht]⟩)

noncomputable def Model.homotopyMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y)
    (hC : C.IsCoherator) (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) :
    X.HomotopyGroup hC x n → Y.HomotopyGroup hC ((underlyingMap f).app 0 x) n :=
  Quot.map (loopsMap f hC x n) (by
    rintro _ _ ⟨h, hs, ht⟩
    exact ⟨(underlyingMap f).app _ h,
      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).source_app, hs]; rfl,
      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).target_app, ht]; rfl⟩)

def Model.WeakEquivalence {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (hC : C.IsCoherator) {X Y : C.Model}
    (f : X ⟶ Y) : Prop :=
  Function.Bijective (componentsMap f) ∧
    ∀ (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ), Function.Bijective (homotopyMap f hC x n)

end GlobularTheory

open CategoryTheory CategoryTheory.Limits

theorem elementary_expansion (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (hC : C.IsCoherator)
    (X : C.Model) (hX : X.IsCellular) (n : ℕ)
    (a : GlobularTheory.Model.disk C n ⟶ X)
    (Y : C.Model) (i : X ⟶ Y)
    (b : GlobularTheory.Model.disk C (n + 1) ⟶ Y)
    (square : IsPushout a (GlobularTheory.Model.J C n) i b) :
    GlobularTheory.Model.WeakEquivalence hC i := by
  sorry

end Grothendieck

end OAI
```

```json
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "face",
      "statement_lean": "def face {m n : ℕ} (h : m < n) (side : Bool) : disk.{u} m ⟶ disk.{u} n :=\n  uliftYoneda.{u}.map (Globe.face h side)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "inductive",
      "name": "Table",
      "statement_lean": "inductive Table : ℕ → Type\n  | globe (n : ℕ) : Table n\n  | attach {last : ℕ} (T : Table last) (b h : ℕ) (left : b < last) (right : b < h) : Table h"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "height",
      "statement_lean": "def height : {n : ℕ} → Table n → ℕ\n  | _, .globe n => n\n  | _, .attach T _ h _ _ => max T.height h"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "realizeWithLast",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def realizeWithLast : {n : ℕ} → Table n →\n    (Σ X : GlobularPresheaf.{0}, GlobularPresheaf.disk n ⟶ X)\n  | _, .globe n => ⟨GlobularPresheaf.disk n, 𝟙 _⟩\n  | _, .attach T _ h hl hr =>\n    let prev := T.realizeWithLast\n    let f := GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ prev.2\n    let g := GlobularPresheaf.face hr false\n    ⟨pushout f g, pushout.inr f g⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "realize",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def realize {n} (T : Table n) : GlobularPresheaf.{0} := T.realizeWithLast.1"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "lastInclusion",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def lastInclusion {n} (T : Table n) : GlobularPresheaf.disk n ⟶ T.realize :=\n  T.realizeWithLast.2"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "abbrev",
      "name": "Shape",
      "statement_lean": "abbrev Shape := Σ n, Table n"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "realize",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def realize (S : Shape) : GlobularPresheaf.{0} := S.2.realize"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "height",
      "statement_lean": "def height (S : Shape) : ℕ := S.2.height"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "globe",
      "statement_lean": "def globe (n : ℕ) : Shape := ⟨n, Table.globe n⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",
      "name": "instance",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable instance : Category Shape where\n  Hom S T := S.realize ⟶ T.realize\n  id S := 𝟙 S.realize\n  comp f g := f ≫ g\n  id_comp := Category.id_comp\n  comp_id := Category.comp_id\n  assoc := Category.assoc"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "face",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def face {m n : ℕ} (h : m < n) (side : Bool) : globe m ⟶ globe n :=\n  GlobularPresheaf.face h side"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "source",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def source (n : ℕ) : globe n ⟶ globe (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) false"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "target",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def target (n : ℕ) : globe n ⟶ globe (n + 1) := face (Nat.lt_succ_self n) true"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "attach",
      "statement_lean": "def attach (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) : Shape :=\n  ⟨h, Table.attach S.2 b h hl hr⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "seam",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def seam (S : Shape) {b : ℕ} (hl : b < S.1) : globe b ⟶ S :=\n  show GlobularPresheaf.disk b ⟶ S.realize from\n    GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "inl",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def inl (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) :\n    S ⟶ S.attach b h hl hr :=\n  pushout.inl (GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion)\n    (GlobularPresheaf.face hr false)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "inr",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def inr (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h) :\n    globe h ⟶ S.attach b h hl hr :=\n  pushout.inr (GlobularPresheaf.face hl true ≫ S.2.lastInclusion)\n    (GlobularPresheaf.face hr false)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "TheoryData",
      "statement_lean": "structure TheoryData where\n  Hom : Shape → Shape → Type v\n  id : ∀ S, Hom S S\n  comp : ∀ {S T U}, Hom S T → Hom T U → Hom S U\n  id_comp : ∀ {S T} (f : Hom S T), comp (id S) f = f\n  comp_id : ∀ {S T} (f : Hom S T), comp f (id T) = f\n  assoc : ∀ {R S T U} (f : Hom R S) (g : Hom S T) (h : Hom T U),\n    comp (comp f g) h = comp f (comp g h)\n  base : ∀ {S T}, (S ⟶ T) → Hom S T\n  base_id : ∀ S, base (𝟙 S) = id S\n  base_comp : ∀ {R S T} (f : R ⟶ S) (g : S ⟶ T), base (f ≫ g) = comp (base f) (base g)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "Obj",
      "statement_lean": "structure Obj (C : TheoryData.{v}) where\n  shape : Shape"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",
      "name": "instance",
      "statement_lean": "instance (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Category C.Obj where\n  Hom S T := C.Hom S.shape T.shape\n  id S := C.id S.shape\n  comp f g := C.comp f g\n  id_comp := C.id_comp\n  comp_id := C.comp_id\n  assoc := C.assoc"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "arity",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def arity (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Shape ⥤ C.Obj where\n  obj S := ⟨S⟩\n  map f := C.base f\n  map_id := C.base_id\n  map_comp := C.base_comp"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "PreservesGlobularSums",
      "statement_lean": "def PreservesGlobularSums (C : TheoryData.{v}) : Prop :=\n  ∀ (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h),\n    IsPushout (C.arity.map (S.seam hl)) (C.arity.map (Shape.face hr false))\n      (C.arity.map (S.inl b h hl hr)) (C.arity.map (S.inr b h hl hr))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "GlobularTheory",
      "statement_lean": "structure GlobularTheory extends TheoryData.{v} where\n  sums : toTheoryData.PreservesGlobularSums"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "abbrev",
      "name": "Obj",
      "statement_lean": "abbrev Obj (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) := C.toTheoryData.Obj\n\n@[ext]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "Morphism",
      "statement_lean": "structure Morphism (C D : GlobularTheory.{v}) where\n  map : ∀ {S T}, C.Hom S T → D.Hom S T\n  map_id : ∀ S, map (C.id S) = D.id S\n  map_comp : ∀ {R S T} (f : C.Hom R S) (g : C.Hom S T),\n    map (C.comp f g) = D.comp (map f) (map g)\n  map_base : ∀ {S T} (f : S ⟶ T), map (C.base f) = D.base f"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "id",
      "statement_lean": "def id (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Morphism C C where\n  map f := f\n  map_id _ := rfl\n  map_comp _ _ := rfl\n  map_base _ := rfl"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "comp",
      "statement_lean": "def comp {C D E : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D) (g : Morphism D E) : Morphism C E where\n  map a := g.map (f.map a)\n  map_id S := by rw [f.map_id, g.map_id]\n  map_comp a b := by rw [f.map_comp, g.map_comp]\n  map_base a := by rw [f.map_base, g.map_base]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Parallel",
      "statement_lean": "def Parallel (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) {S : Shape} :\n    ∀ n, C.Hom (Shape.globe n) S → C.Hom (Shape.globe n) S → Prop\n  | 0, _, _ => True\n  | n + 1, f, g =>\n    C.comp (C.base (Shape.source n)) f = C.comp (C.base (Shape.source n)) g ∧\n    C.comp (C.base (Shape.target n)) f = C.comp (C.base (Shape.target n)) g"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "AdmissiblePair",
      "statement_lean": "structure AdmissiblePair (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where\n  n : ℕ\n  arity : Shape\n  height : arity.height ≤ n + 1\n  source : C.Hom (Shape.globe n) arity\n  target : C.Hom (Shape.globe n) arity\n  parallel : C.Parallel n source target"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Fills",
      "statement_lean": "def Fills {C D : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D) (p : C.AdmissiblePair)\n    (z : D.Hom (Shape.globe (p.n + 1)) p.arity) : Prop :=\n  D.comp (D.base (Shape.source p.n)) z = f.map p.source ∧\n  D.comp (D.base (Shape.target p.n)) z = f.map p.target"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsFreeExtension",
      "statement_lean": "def IsFreeExtension {C D : GlobularTheory.{v}} (f : Morphism C D)\n    (G : Type v) (pair : G → C.AdmissiblePair)\n    (filler : ∀ g, D.Hom (Shape.globe ((pair g).n + 1)) (pair g).arity) : Prop :=\n  (∀ g, Fills f (pair g) (filler g)) ∧\n  ∀ (E : GlobularTheory.{v}) (a : Morphism C E)\n    (chosen : ∀ g, E.Hom (Shape.globe ((pair g).n + 1)) (pair g).arity),\n    (∀ g, Fills a (pair g) (chosen g)) →\n    ∃! b : Morphism D E, f.comp b = a ∧ ∀ g, b.map (filler g) = chosen g"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "CellularPresentation",
      "statement_lean": "structure CellularPresentation (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where\n  stage : ℕ → GlobularTheory.{v}\n  initial : ∀ D : GlobularTheory.{v}, Nonempty (Unique (Morphism (stage 0) D))\n  step : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) (stage (r + 1))\n  generators : ℕ → Type v\n  pair : ∀ r, generators r → (stage r).AdmissiblePair\n  filler : ∀ r g, (stage (r + 1)).Hom (Shape.globe ((pair r g).n + 1)) (pair r g).arity\n  free : ∀ r, IsFreeExtension (step r) (generators r) (pair r) (filler r)\n  inclusion : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) C\n  compatible : ∀ r, (step r).comp (inclusion (r + 1)) = inclusion r\n  colimit : ∀ (D : GlobularTheory.{v}) (a : ∀ r, Morphism (stage r) D),\n    (∀ r, (step r).comp (a (r + 1)) = a r) →\n    ∃! b : Morphism C D, ∀ r, (inclusion r).comp b = a r"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsCoherator",
      "statement_lean": "def IsCoherator (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Prop :=\n  Nonempty C.CellularPresentation ∧\n  ∀ p : C.AdmissiblePair, ∃ z, Fills (Morphism.id C) p z"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "shapeGlobes",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def shapeGlobes : Globe ⥤ Shape where\n  obj n := Shape.globe n.dim\n  map f := uliftYoneda.map f\n  map_id n := by exact uliftYoneda.map_id n\n  map_comp f g := by exact uliftYoneda.map_comp f g"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "globes",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def globes (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Globe ⥤ C.Obj :=\n  shapeGlobes ⋙ C.toTheoryData.arity"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "Model",
      "statement_lean": "structure Model (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) where\n  presheaf : C.Objᵒᵖ ⥤ Type v\n  sums : ∀ (S : Shape) (b h : ℕ) (hl : b < S.1) (hr : b < h),\n    IsPullback\n      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.inl b h hl hr)).op)\n      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.inr b h hl hr)).op)\n      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (S.seam hl)).op)\n      (presheaf.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (Shape.face hr false)).op)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",
      "name": "instance",
      "statement_lean": "instance (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) : Category C.Model where\n  Hom X Y := X.presheaf ⟶ Y.presheaf\n  id X := 𝟙 X.presheaf\n  comp f g := f ≫ g\n  id_comp := Category.id_comp\n  comp_id := Category.comp_id\n  assoc := Category.assoc"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "underlying",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def underlying {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : GlobularSet.{v} :=\n  GlobularPresheaf.underlying (C.globes.op ⋙ X.presheaf)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "underlyingMap",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def underlyingMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) :\n    X.underlying.Hom Y.underlying :=\n  GlobularPresheaf.underlyingMap (Functor.whiskerLeft C.globes.op f)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "freeShape",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def freeShape (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (S : Shape) : C.Model where\n  presheaf := yoneda.obj ⟨S⟩\n  sums T b h hl hr := ((C.sums T b h hl hr).op.map (yoneda.obj ⟨S⟩)).flip"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "disk",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def disk (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : C.Model :=\n  freeShape C (Shape.globe n)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "J",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def J (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : disk C n ⟶ disk C (n + 1) :=\n  show (disk C n).presheaf ⟶ (disk C (n + 1)).presheaf from\n    yoneda.map (C.toTheoryData.arity.map (Shape.source n))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "BoundaryAttachment",
      "statement_lean": "structure BoundaryAttachment {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) where\n  index : Type v\n  dimension : index → ℕ\n  boundary : ∀ k, X.underlying.Boundary (dimension k)\n  cell : ∀ k, Y.underlying.Cell (dimension k)\n  cell_boundary : ∀ k, Y.underlying.boundary _ (cell k) =\n    (underlyingMap f).boundaryMap _ (boundary k)\n  universal : ∀ (Z : C.Model) (g : X ⟶ Z)\n    (z : ∀ k, Z.underlying.Cell (dimension k)),\n    (∀ k, Z.underlying.boundary _ (z k) = (underlyingMap g).boundaryMap _ (boundary k)) →\n    ∃! h : Y ⟶ Z, f ≫ h = g ∧ ∀ k, (underlyingMap h).app _ (cell k) = z k"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "CellularPresentation",
      "statement_lean": "structure CellularPresentation {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) where\n  order : Type v\n  linearOrder : LinearOrder order\n  orderBot : OrderBot order\n  succOrder : SuccOrder order\n  wellFoundedLT : WellFoundedLT order\n  initial : C.Model\n  isInitial : IsInitial initial\n  start : initial ⟶ X\n  chain : @TransfiniteCompositionOfShape _ _ order linearOrder orderBot\n    _ _ start succOrder wellFoundedLT\n  attachment : ∀ j, ¬ IsMax j → BoundaryAttachment\n    (chain.F.map (homOfLE (Order.le_succ j)))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsCellular",
      "statement_lean": "def IsCellular {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : Prop := Nonempty X.CellularPresentation"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "unitPair",
      "statement_lean": "def unitPair (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (n : ℕ) : C.AdmissiblePair where\n  n := n\n  arity := Shape.globe n\n  height := Nat.le_succ n\n  source := C.id (Shape.globe n)\n  target := C.id (Shape.globe n)\n  parallel := by cases n <;> simp [Parallel]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "unit",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def unit (C : GlobularTheory.{v}) (hC : C.IsCoherator) (n : ℕ) :\n    C.Hom (Shape.globe (n + 1)) (Shape.globe n) := Classical.choose (hC.2 (C.unitPair n))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.eval",
      "statement_lean": "def Model.eval {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) {S T : Shape} (f : C.Hom S T) :\n    X.presheaf.obj (op (⟨T⟩ : C.Obj)) → X.presheaf.obj (op (⟨S⟩ : C.Obj)) :=\n  X.presheaf.map (show (⟨S⟩ : C.Obj) ⟶ ⟨T⟩ from f).op"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.unitCell",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def Model.unitCell {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)\n    (n : ℕ) (x : X.underlying.Cell n) : X.underlying.Cell (n + 1) :=\n  X.eval (C.unit hC n) x"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.baseCell",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def Model.baseCell {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)\n    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) : ∀ n, X.underlying.Cell n\n  | 0 => x\n  | n + 1 => X.unitCell hC n (X.baseCell hC x n)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.Loops",
      "statement_lean": "def Model.Loops {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)\n    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) : Type v :=\n  { z : X.underlying.Cell (n + 1) // X.underlying.source n z = X.baseCell hC x n ∧\n      X.underlying.target n z = X.baseCell hC x n }"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.Components",
      "statement_lean": "def Model.Components {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) : Type v :=\n  Quot (X.underlying.Joined 0)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.HomotopyGroup",
      "statement_lean": "def Model.HomotopyGroup {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} (X : C.Model) (hC : C.IsCoherator)\n    (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) : Type v :=\n  Quot (fun a b : X.Loops hC x n => X.underlying.Joined (n + 1) a.1 b.1)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.loopsMap",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def Model.loopsMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y)\n    (hC : C.IsCoherator) (x : X.underlying.Cell 0) (n : ℕ) :\n    X.Loops hC x n → Y.Loops hC ((underlyingMap f).app 0 x) n := by\n  have hb : ∀ k, (underlyingMap f).app k (X.baseCell hC x k) =\n      Y.baseCell hC ((underlyingMap f).app 0 x) k := by\n    intro k\n    induction k with\n    | zero => rfl\n    | succ k ih =>\n      have h := ConcreteCategory.congr_hom (f.naturality\n        (show (op (⟨Shape.globe k⟩ : C.Obj)) ⟶ op ⟨Shape.globe (k+1)⟩ from\n          Quiver.Hom.op (C.unit hC k))) (X.baseCell hC x k)\n      change (underlyingMap f).app (k+1) (X.unitCell hC k (X.baseCell hC x k)) =\n        Y.unitCell hC k ((underlyingMap f).app k (X.baseCell hC x k)) at h\n      simpa only [baseCell, ih] using h\n  exact fun z => ⟨(underlyingMap f).app (n + 1) z.1,\n    by rw [← (underlyingMap f).source_app, z.2.1, hb],\n    by rw [← (underlyingMap f).target_app, z.2.2, hb]⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.componentsMap",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def Model.componentsMap {C : GlobularTheory.{v}} {X Y : C.Model} (f : X ⟶ Y) :\n    X.Components → Y.Components :=\n  Quot.map ((underlyingMap f).app 0) (by\n    rintro _ _ ⟨h, hs, ht⟩\n    exact ⟨(underlyingMap f).app _ h,\n      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).source_app, hs],\n      by rw [← (underlyingMap f).target_app, ht]⟩)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Model.homotopyMap",
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Grothendieck homotopy hypothesis for every Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention: its weak globular infinity-groupoids recover the homotopy theory of spaces. We also resolve Henry's pushout conjecture, showing that elementary expansions preserve components and all homotopy groups of cellular infinity-groupoids.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Grothendieck’s homotopy hypothesis asks whether weak globular infinity-groupoids describe the homotopy theory of spaces. In *Pursuing Stacks*, the operations on paths and higher homotopies are specified by freely adjoining coherence operations to globular pasting diagrams (Grothendieck 1983, sec. 9 and 12–13) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Grothendieck1983](#ref-Grothendieck1983)]. A *coherator* records these choices. Maltsiniotis gave a precise formulation in terms of coherators and their models, together with realization and fundamental-infinity-groupoid functors (Maltsiniotis 2007, 2010) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Maltsiniotis2007](#ref-Maltsiniotis2007), [Maltsiniotis2010](#ref-Maltsiniotis2010)]. The resulting categories depend on the chosen coherator; the hypothesis asserts that their homotopy theory recovers that of spaces. We establish it for every coherator in the Ara–Henry convention specified below.

The choice of algebraic model is substantive. Simplicial sets already model spaces through the classical realization–singular-complex Quillen equivalence (Hovey 1999, Theorem 3.6.7) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Hovey1999](#ref-Hovey1999)]. For strict globular infinity-groupoids, however, the simply connected homotopy types represented under a realization preserving homotopy groups are precisely products of Eilenberg–Mac Lane spaces (Ara 2013b, Theorem 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AraStrict2013](#ref-AraStrict2013)]. The passage from strict identities to weak coherence is therefore substantive. Here weak equivalences are defined inside the chosen globular algebraic category, using components and based homotopy groups.

Henry isolated a concrete obstruction to constructing their canonical semi-model structure (Henry 2016, Conjecture 5.3.3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. Suppose that $X$ is built by attaching globular cells. Select an $n$-cell $a$ of $X$, adjoin a new parallel $n$-cell $a'$, and adjoin an $(n+1)$-cell from $a$ to $a'$. The conjecture asserts that this free extension is a weak equivalence. Although the extension has an elementary retraction, its free algebraic construction also creates composites and higher coherence cells. Controlling the homotopy classes of all those cells is the issue.

We use Grothendieck coherators in the convention of Ara (Ara 2013a, sec. 2.3 and 2.9–2.11) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ara2013](#ref-Ara2013)], fixed by Henry in (Henry 2016, sec. 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]; the precise definition appears in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>. Thus generating fillers of output dimension $j$ have pasting-scheme arity of dimension at most $j$. Write $D_j$ for the free model on a $j$-globe and $\partial D_j$ for its boundary, with $\partial D_0=\varnothing$. A model is *cellular* if it is obtained from the initial model by a possibly transfinite sequence of boundary attachments. Weak equivalences induce a bijection on components and isomorphisms on all based homotopy groups.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Fix any Grothendieck coherator $\mathcal C$. Let $X$ be a cellular $\mathcal C$-infinity-groupoid, let $n\ge0$, and let $a:D_n\to X$ be any cell. In the pushout square $$\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center),
                   node distance=1.25cm and 2.2cm,>=Stealth]
\node (a) {\(D_n\)};
\node (b) [right=of a] {\(D_{n+1}\)};
\node (c) [below=of a] {\(X\)};
\node (d) at (b |- c) {\(X^+\)};
\draw[->] (a) -- node[above] {\(s_n\)} (b);
\draw[->] (a) -- node[left] {\(a\)} (c);
\draw[->] (b) -- (d);
\draw[->] (c) -- node[below] {\(i\)} (d);
\end{tikzpicture}$$ where $s_n$ is the source inclusion, the map $i:X\to X^+$ is a weak equivalence.*

</div>

</div>

The pushout is taken in models of the fixed theory $\mathcal C$. The selected cell $a$ can be any composite or coherence cell. For $n=0$, the extension adjoins an object and an arrow from the selected object to it. In particular, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves Henry’s pushout conjecture affirmatively. Henry already showed how its finitely cellular case extends to transfinite cellular objects and their cofibrant retracts (Henry 2016, Lemma 5.3.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]; the proof here treats arbitrary cellular objects directly. The canonical semi-model structure and its equivalence with the homotopy theory of spaces follow in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">5</a>.

The algebraic and homotopical sides of this question developed together. Berger’s globular theories organize operations by tree-shaped pasting arities (Berger 2002, Definition 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Berger2002](#ref-Berger2002)]. Ara constructed the homotopy-group calculus and fixed-boundary tests for weak equivalences used here (Ara 2013a, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Ara2013](#ref-Ara2013)]. Bourke described cellular globular theories through iterated algebraic injectivity and proved faithfulness of their free stages (Bourke 2020, Theorems 3.12 and 5.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Bourke2020](#ref-Bourke2020)]. These results make the algebraic models and their weak equivalences precise; the remaining task is to show that elementary expansions have the required homotopical behavior.

Henry identified the elementary pushout assertion as sufficient both for the canonical semi-model structure and for the comparison with spaces (Henry 2016, Theorem 5.3.5 and Corollary 5.3.13) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Henry2016](#ref-Henry2016)]. One route to this assertion is a functorial path construction. Bourke’s endomorphism-theory argument puts weak groupoid structures on iterated path objects in identity type categories (Bourke 2016, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Bourke2016](#ref-Bourke2016)]. Within Grothendieck groupoids, Lanari constructed a globular candidate with compositions, identities and inverses (Lanari 2020, Definitions 5.2 and 6.1, Proposition 6.2, and Theorem 6.5) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Lanari2020](#ref-Lanari2020)]. His construction leaves the extension to compatible interpretations of all higher operations open. The partial paths used here are adapted to one expansion index at a time: the already established higher-index expansions make their representing objects contractible, which then permits every operation of the fixed coherator to be interpreted in its original free order.

In dimension three, Lanari obtained the canonical semi-model structure for Grothendieck groupoids of a suitable type (Lanari 2018, Theorem 4.10) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Lanari2018](#ref-Lanari2018)], and Henry and Lanari proved the corresponding homotopy-hypothesis comparison (Henry and Lanari 2023) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[HenryLanari2023](#ref-HenryLanari2023)]. Their study of coskeletal and truncated globular models also supplies context for the cutoffs used below (Henry and Lanari 2023, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[HenryLanari2023](#ref-HenryLanari2023)]. Taylor’s later construction of algebraic coherators and Grothendieck realizations retains a generalized pushout conjecture as a hypothesis (Taylor 2026, Conjecture 5.7 and Theorem 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Taylor2026](#ref-Taylor2026)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves Henry’s assertion for an arbitrary fixed coherator in our convention. Its consequence in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">5</a> is a Quillen equivalence with spaces, and hence independence of the resulting homotopy theory from that choice of coherator.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The proof
